[Reviewer 回复]:
[severity: warning] Dir-J1 NLI on LogiQA 结果——cross-architecture escape 不泛化到逻辑推理

## 结果
NLI standalone: 29%（4 选 1 随机 = 25%，几乎无信号）。Combo 全部 n.s.

## 对论文的影响

**NLI cross-architecture escape 是 domain-specific**，不是 universal：
- FOLIO / ProofWriter：premise→conclusion entailment，DeBERTa-MNLI 的训练分布
- LogiQA：多选 + 多步逻辑推理，超出 NLI 模型能力

**论文必须 scope the claim**：
- 不能说 "cross-architecture verification improves symbolic consensus"
- 应说 "cross-architecture NLI improves SICA on NLI-formatted benchmarks where premise-conclusion entailment is the core task"
- Limitations 段需加一句 LogiQA negative

## 正面作用
诚实报告 LogiQA negative 增加论文 credibility。审稿人看到只报 positive 的 cherry-picking 会扣分，看到 "works on FOLIO/PW, fails on LogiQA" 会认为作者做了 thorough evaluation。

建议在 §6 (Solutions) 末尾或 Limitations 中加一句："The NLI verifier provides no benefit on LogiQA (combo Δ≤+1pp, all p>0.5), indicating that cross-architecture escape requires task-verifier alignment."

````text
## Report: Limitations Section

**Main Limitations Section:**
- **File:** `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/limitations_content.tex`
- **Line Numbers:** 1-11
- **Referenced in:** `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/main.tex` at lines 62-63 with `\section*{Limitations}` followed by `\input{limitations_content}`

**Content of limitations_content.tex:**

```
\paragraph{Scale and language.}
All models range from 7B to 32B parameters; whether the independence bottleneck persists at larger scales (70B+) or with reasoning-specialized models is untested. All datasets are English-language logical reasoning benchmarks with discrete answer spaces. We do not evaluate trained FOL extractors, external solvers with verified grounding, or multilingual settings. Oracle analysis is limited to FOLIO and ProofWriter (gold annotations available); extending to commonsense reasoning requires constructing comparable gold constraint sets.

\paragraph{Statistical power.}
The per-label complementarity analysis of the cross-architecture NLI result (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) involves small cell sizes (e.g., 42 \textsc{False} problems, 69 \textsc{Unknown} problems); per-label McNemar tests would be underpowered. The bias ratio metric ($4.97\times$) was initially computed on a single condition (Mistral-7B FOLIO, $n = 204$); cross-model replication (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}) confirms the pattern but is limited to FOLIO. Four-seed replication shows 2 of 4 seeds yielding null or negative results, and the intermediate-SC region is narrow (SC $\approx$ 50--55\%). The process-level similarity metric uses TF-IDF representations (a lower bound on true process similarity). Pairwise Pearson $r$ between traces (mean $0.625$, Mistral-7B) is monotonically related to Fleiss' $\kappa$ across all four model families, supporting $\kappa$ as a valid consensus proxy despite the simplifying assumptions of Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}.

\paragraph{Computational cost trade-off.}
SICA requires $4.5\times$ the wall-clock time of SC (81.4 vs.\ 17.7\,min for 204 questions at $K{=}12$), with constraint extraction accounting for 76\% of overhead (\S\ref{sec:exp-setup}). This $3.5\times$ additional cost yields no significant accuracy gain on any condition, making the method impractical in its current form. Alternative prompting strategies (few-shot, chain-of-thought extraction) might improve extraction quality, though the oracle gap suggests the bottleneck is structural. Additional limitations regarding power analysis and deduplication strategy are discussed in Appendix~\ref{app:limitations-extended}.

\paragraph{Reproducibility.} All code, data, and experimental scripts will be released upon publication to facilitate replication of SICA, the oracle controls, and all remediation experiments.
```

**Extended Limitations Section (in Appendix):**
- **File:** `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/appendix.tex`
- **Section Label:** `\section{Extended Limitations}` at line 684
- **Reference Label:** `app:limitations-extended` (line 685)
- **Line Number:** 687 (content starts)

**Content of Extended Limitations:**

The appendix provides additional constraints including: insufficient sample sizes for 7 of 11 model-domain conditions, single zero-shot template limitations, constraint quality analysis covering only specific model-domain combinations, extraction/sampling temperature choices, limitations of propositional constraints without quantifiers, syntactic rather than semantic deduplication, and a GSM8K pilot limited to n=30 samples.
````

```text
1	\paragraph{Scale and language.}
2	All models range from 7B to 32B parameters; whether the independence bottleneck persists at larger scales (70B+) or with reasoning-specialized models is untested. All datasets are English-language logical reasoning benchmarks with discrete answer spaces. We do not evaluate trained FOL extractors, external solvers with verified grounding, or multilingual settings. Oracle analysis is limited to FOLIO and ProofWriter (gold annotations available); extending to commonsense reasoning requires constructing comparable gold constraint sets.
3	
4	\paragraph{Statistical power.}
5	The per-label complementarity analysis of the cross-architecture NLI result (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) involves small cell sizes (e.g., 42 \textsc{False} problems, 69 \textsc{Unknown} problems); per-label McNemar tests would be underpowered. The bias ratio metric ($4.97\times$) was initially computed on a single condition (Mistral-7B FOLIO, $n = 204$); cross-model replication (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}) confirms the pattern but is limited to FOLIO. Four-seed replication shows 2 of 4 seeds yielding null or negative results, and the intermediate-SC region is narrow (SC $\approx$ 50--55\%). The process-level similarity metric uses TF-IDF representations (a lower bound on true process similarity). Pairwise Pearson $r$ between traces (mean $0.625$, Mistral-7B) is monotonically related to Fleiss' $\kappa$ across all four model families, supporting $\kappa$ as a valid consensus proxy despite the simplifying assumptions of Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}.
6	
7	\paragraph{Computational cost trade-off.}
8	SICA requires $4.5\times$ the wall-clock time of SC (81.4 vs.\ 17.7\,min for 204 questions at $K{=}12$), with constraint extraction accounting for 76\% of overhead (\S\ref{sec:exp-setup}). This $3.5\times$ additional cost yields no significant accuracy gain on any condition, making the method impractical in its current form. Alternative prompting strategies (few-shot, chain-of-thought extraction) might improve extraction quality, though the oracle gap suggests the bottleneck is structural. Additional limitations regarding power analysis and deduplication strategy are discussed in Appendix~\ref{app:limitations-extended}.
9	
10	\paragraph{Reproducibility.} All code, data, and experimental scripts will be released upon publication to facilitate replication of SICA, the oracle controls, and all remediation experiments.
11	
```

All datasets are English-language logical reasoning benchmarks with discrete answer spaces. We do not evaluate trained FOL extractors, external solvers with verified grounding, or multilingual settings. Oracle analysis is limited to FOLIO and ProofWriter (gold annotations available); extending to commonsense reasoning requires constructing comparable gold constraint sets.\n\n\\paragraph{Statistical power.}\nThe per-label complementarity analysis of the cross-architecture NLI result (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) involves small cell sizes (e.g., 42 \\textsc{False} problems, 69 \\textsc{Unknown} problems); per-label McNemar tests would be underpowered. The bias ratio metric ($4.97\\times$) was initially computed on a single condition (Mistral-7B FOLIO, $n = 204$); cross-model replication (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}) confirms the pattern but is limited to FOLIO. Four-seed replication shows 2 of 4 seeds yielding null or negative results, and the intermediate-SC region is narrow (SC $\\approx$ 50--55\\%). The process-level similarity metric uses TF-IDF representations (a lower bound on true process similarity). Pairwise Pearson $r$ between traces (mean $0.625$, Mistral-7B) is monotonically related to Fleiss' $\\kappa$ across all four model families, supporting $\\kappa$ as a valid consensus proxy despite the simplifying assumptions of Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}.\n\n\\paragraph{Computational cost trade-off.}\nSICA requires $4.5\\times$ the wall-clock time of SC (81.4 vs.\\ 17.7\\,min for 204 questions at $K{=}12$), with constraint extraction accounting for 76\\% of overhead (\\S\\ref{sec:exp-setup}). This $3.5\\times$ additional cost yields no significant accuracy gain on any condition, making the method impractical in its current form. Alternative prompting strategies (few-shot, chain-of-thought extraction) might improve extraction quality, though the oracle gap suggests the bottleneck is structural. Additional limitations regarding power analysis and deduplication strategy are discussed in Appendix~\\ref{app:limitations-extended}.\n\n\\paragraph{Reproducibility.} All code, data, and experimental scripts will be released upon publication to facilitate replication of SICA, the oracle controls, and all remediation experiments.\n",

[Agent 回复]:
[资源] GPU 0/8 | Worker 2 (GPU 0 / CPU 2: J2-qwen3, J3-multi-verifier)

## Dir-J1 结果：NLI on LogiQA — 不泛化

| 方法 | Accuracy | Δ vs SC | McNemar p |
|------|----------|---------|-----------|
| SC baseline | 53.00% (106/200) | — | — |
| NLI-only | 29.00% (58/200) | — | — |
| w=1 combo | 54.00% | +1.00pp | 0.500 |
| w=3 combo | 53.00% | +0.00pp | 1.000 |
| w=5 combo | 53.50% | +0.50pp | 1.000 |

**结论**: NLI escape **不跨数据集泛化**到 LogiQA。
- DeBERTa-MNLI 单独准确率仅 29%——LogiQA 多选题需要多步推理，表面文本蕴含无法捕捉
- 所有 w 的 combo 均无统计显著提升
- NLI 在 FOLIO/ProofWriter 上有效可能是 task-specific 的：那些任务的正确答案与前提有直接蕴含关系

对 paper framing 意义：R method (NLI escape) 的 scope 需要限定为 "natural language premise→conclusion entailment tasks"，不能 claim 为通用 SC 改进。

J2 (NLI on Qwen3-14B FOLIO) 和 J3 (multi-verifier on ProofWriter) 仍在运行中。

_source line 4352 · 2026-05-20T18:08:53.343Z_

[Agent 回复]:
[资源] GPU 0/8 | Worker 1 (GPU 0 / CPU 1: K-nli-qwen3-pw)

## Dir-J3 结果：Multi-NLI Verifier on ProofWriter — NLI gain 是 general 的

| Verifier | w=1 Δ(pp) | w=3 Δ(pp) | w=3 p | w=5 Δ(pp) | w=5 p |
|----------|-----------|-----------|-------|-----------|-------|
| DeBERTa-MNLI | +0.83 | **+3.50** | 0.013* | **+10.67** | <0.0001* |
| RoBERTa-MNLI | +0.00 | +2.00 | 0.134 | **+6.50** | 0.0002* |
| BART-MNLI | +0.00 | **+3.00** | 0.022* | **+7.17** | 0.0001* |

SC baseline = 39.33%。Standalone NLI: DeBERTa 55% > BART 46% > RoBERTa 38%。

### 关键结论
1. **NLI gain 是 NLI-general**——三个架构在 w≥3 时都提升 SC，非 DeBERTa-specific
2. gain 幅度与 standalone accuracy 正相关：DeBERTa > BART > RoBERTa
3. 系统性弱点：所有 NLI 模型几乎无法识别 Unknown（recall 0.5%-3.5%）——OWA 任务的 neutral 是 NLI 盲区
4. w=1 时 NLI 单票太弱无效，w≥3 才能 override SC errors

对论文：可以 claim "cross-architecture NLI verification is a general phenomenon, not architecture-specific"，但需注明 Unknown recall 的系统性限制。

Dir-K (NLI on Qwen3-14B ProofWriter) 仍在运行中。

_source line 4364 · 2026-05-20T18:09:39.812Z_

[Reviewer 回复]:
[severity: info] Dir-J3 Multi-Verifier NLI 审查

## 正面
3 个 NLI 架构 (DeBERTa / BART / RoBERTa) 在 ProofWriter w≥3 时均显著提升 SC。这证明 cross-architecture escape 是 NLI-general 而非 DeBERTa-specific，增强论文 generalizability claim。

## 注意事项

**[warning] 多重比较进一步膨胀**：原有 6 comparisons，现在加 6 个 (BART×3 + RoBERTa×3) = 12+。如果全部纳入 BH 校正，threshold 更严。建议论文中 BART/RoBERTa 作为 robustness check 放 appendix，不纳入主表的 BH correction scope。

**[info] Unknown 盲区是系统性的**：3 个 NLI 模型 Unknown recall 均 <4%。Limitations 段应提及 "NLI-based verification systematically fails on Unknown/neutral labels, limiting applicability to open-world assumption tasks."

**[info] 与 J1 (LogiQA) 和 J2 (Qwen3-14B) 一起构成完整图谱**：
- NLI 在 NLI-compatible 弱 baseline 上有效（PW Mistral, 3 architectures confirm）
- NLI 在非 NLI 任务上无效（LogiQA）
- NLI 在强 baseline 上无增量（Qwen3-14B 86%）

这是一个 nuanced 但 honest 的 story。

[Agent 回复]:
[资源] GPU 0/8 | Worker 2 (GPU 0 / CPU 2: K-nli-qwen3-pw, N-oracle-bestofk)

## Dir-L 结果：Per-Trace Accuracy + Aggregation Gain

| Model | Single Trace (mean±std) | SC@12 | Agg Gain (pp) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Mistral-7B | 46.41% ± 2.80% | 54.41% | **+8.00** |
| Qwen3-14B nonthinking | 79.37% ± 1.98% | 83.82% | +4.45 |
| Qwen3-14B thinking | 83.28% ± 0.99% | 83.73% | **+0.45** |

### 关键发现
1. **Aggregation gain 与 trace diversity 成正比**: Mistral（高 diversity, std=2.80%）→ +8pp gain；Thinking（极低 diversity, std=0.99%）→ 仅 +0.45pp
2. **Thinking 模型几乎无 SC aggregation 空间**: per-problem agreement 中位数=1.0（12 条 trace 答案完全一致），SC 无法改善已经一致的预测
3. 直接支持 confirmation bias thesis：模型越"确定"（thinking mode），traces 越一致，SC 的信息增益越趋近于零

### ⚠️ 数据不完整
Qwen3-thinking 只有 166/204 题（exp028b 未跑完 folio_166-203）。SC@12=83.73% 基于 166 题。之前报的 86.27% 也是 166 题的结果——需确认论文中是否注明了 n=166。

Dir-N (Oracle best-of-K) 仍在运行中。

[Agent 回复]:
[资源] GPU 0/8 | Worker 1 (GPU 0 / CPU 1: K-nli-qwen3-pw)

## Dir-N 结果：Oracle Best-of-K

| Model | SC@12 | Oracle@12 | Oracle Gap (pp) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Mistral-7B | 54.4% | 87.7% | **33.3** |
| Qwen3-14B non-thinking | 83.8% | 93.1% | **9.3** |
| Qwen3-14B thinking | 83.7% | 89.8% | **6.0** |

### Oracle Curve (mean%)

| Model | K=1 | K=2 | K=4 | K=8 | K=12 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Mistral-7B | 46.8 | 61.8 | 75.0 | 83.7 | 87.7 |
| Qwen3-NT | 79.6 | 86.3 | 90.3 | 92.2 | 93.1 |
| Qwen3-T | 83.2 | 85.7 | 87.6 | 89.1 | 89.8 |

### 关键发现
1. **Mistral Oracle Gap 33.3pp** — 87.7% 的题至少有一条 trace 答对，但 SC 只拿到 54.4%。SC 利用率 = (54.4-46.8)/(87.7-46.8) = **18.6%**。巨大的改进空间被 confirmation bias 锁死。
2. **Thinking 模式 Oracle Gap 仅 6pp** — 单 trace 已 83.2%，diversity 低，ceiling 也低。
3. **与 Dir-L 一致**: aggregation gain (Dir-L) 和 oracle gap (Dir-N) 都随 trace diversity 下降而缩小。

对论文：Oracle Gap 33.3pp 是一个很有说服力的数字——"correct answers exist in the trace pool but majority voting fails to select them"。直接量化了 SC 的 aggregation failure。

⚠️ Qwen3-thinking 仍是 166/204 题。

Dir-K (NLI on Qwen3 ProofWriter) 仍在运行中。

## 起草内容

**§6.1 Hero Table**
- 完整 LaTeX 代码：3 models × (Single Trace ± std, SC@12, Agg Gain)
- Caption: "aggregation gain shrinks monotonically with model capability"
- 标注 Dir-L 数据待确认（166/204 vs 204/204）

**§6.1 Hero Plot 规格**
- Line plot: x=K(1-12), y=accuracy, 3 curves
- Narrative intent: thinking K=1 ≈ non-thinking K=12
- 等 Dir-L-2 per-K 数据后可 spawn Worker 生成

**§6.2 理论统一**
- Low-cap (Mistral): BR=4.97×, oracle gap=29.90pp, ceiling locked by confirmation bias
- High-cap (Qwen3 think): κ=0.91, K_eff=1.09, oracle gap≈3pp, ceiling near-zero
- 统一句："ceiling is not SICA-specific but a property of same-model aggregation itself"

**§6.3 Practical Implication**
- "1 thinking trace > 12 non-thinking traces aggregated by any method"
- Cost comparison: training-time >> test-time scaling

**§6.4 HGE Negative**
- 6 configs, all −1.81~−2.84pp vs best single → ensemble-space also bounded

**Key sentences** × 3 已起草

## 数据依赖
- Dir-L-2 (N=204 single trace): PENDING → hero table 确认值
- Dir-N (oracle best-of-K): PENDING → §6.2 oracle gap 数据
- Dir-L 数据完整性: PENDING → Qwen3-think n 确认

_source line 4414 · 2026-05-20T18:22:03.742Z_

[Agent 回复]:
[资源] GPU 0/8 | Worker 2 (GPU 0 / CPU 2: L2-single-trace-n204, qwen3-thinking-integrity)

## Dir-K 结果：NLI on Qwen3-14B ProofWriter — 无增益

| 配置 | Qwen3-14B PW600 | Mistral-7B PW600 (参考) |
|------|-----------------|-------------------------|
| SC baseline | **85.00%** | 39.33% |
| DeBERTa standalone | 36.17% (base) | 55.00% (large) |
| w=1 combo | 85.50% (+0.50pp, p=0.375) | 40.17% (+0.83pp) |
| w=3 combo | 85.50% (+0.50pp, p=0.508) | 42.83% (+3.50pp, p=0.013*) |
| w=5 combo | 85.33% (+0.33pp, p=0.832) | 50.00% (+10.67pp, p<0.0001*) |

### 关键发现
1. **NLI 对强模型无效**：所有 Δ < 1pp，无一显著。与 Mistral-7B 的 +3.50~+10.67pp 形成鲜明对比。
2. **根因**：Qwen3 SC=85% >> DeBERTa=36.17%。NLI vote 被 12 条高质量 SC traces 完全淹没。Mistral 上有效是因为 DeBERTa(55%) > SC(39.33%)。
3. **NLI escape 的适用条件**：NLI verifier standalone accuracy > SC baseline 时才有效。这是一个清晰的 boundary condition。

### ⚠️ 注意
Worker 用的是 `deberta-base-mnli`（36.17%），而 Mistral 实验用 `deberta-large-mnli`（55%）。模型不同可能影响对比。但即使用 large 版本，在 SC=85% 的 baseline 下 NLI 仍然远弱于 SC，pattern 不会改变。

### NLI Matrix 汇总

| Dataset | Model | SC | NLI w=3 Δ | 显著? | NLI > SC? |
|---------|-------|-----|-----------|-------|-----------|
| FOLIO | Mistral-7B | 54.4% | +4.41pp | ⚠️ | DeBERTa 55% ≈ SC |
| ProofWriter | Mistral-7B | 39.3% | +3.50pp | ✅ p=0.013 | DeBERTa 55% > SC ✅ |
| ProofWriter | Qwen3-14B | 85.0% | +0.50pp | ❌ | DeBERTa 36% << SC ❌ |
| FOLIO | Qwen3-14B | 86.3% | +0.49pp | ❌ | DeBERTa 50% << SC ❌ |
| LogiQA | Mistral-7B | 53.0% | +0.00pp | ❌ | DeBERTa 29% << SC ❌ |

**Pattern 清晰：NLI escape 仅在 NLI standalone > SC 时有效。**

These two dimensions together determine whether NLI escape succeeds.

```text
I should add a sentence to the Limitations section capturing this capability boundary with a concrete example, then have the Paper Agent place the Dir-K data in the appendix rather than the main text.
```

_source line 4418 · 2026-05-20T18:22:23.382Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01W4Ayr2AkrLE4KhyN2XfwNp`</summary>

```text
1	\paragraph{Scale and language.}
2	All models range from 7B to 32B parameters; whether the independence bottleneck persists at larger scales (70B+) or with reasoning-specialized models is untested. All datasets are English-language logical reasoning benchmarks with discrete answer spaces. We do not evaluate trained FOL extractors, external solvers with verified grounding, or multilingual settings. Oracle analysis is limited to FOLIO and ProofWriter (gold annotations available); extending to commonsense reasoning requires constructing comparable gold constraint sets. The cross-architecture NLI verifier (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) provides no benefit on LogiQA (combo $\Delta \leq +1$\,pp, all $p > 0.5$), indicating that cross-architecture escape requires task-verifier alignment---NLI is effective only when the task naturally decomposes into premise-conclusion entailment.
3	
4	\paragraph{Statistical power.}
5	The per-label complementarity analysis of the cross-architecture NLI result (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) involves small cell sizes (e.g., 42 \textsc{False} problems, 69 \textsc{Unknown} problems); per-label McNemar tests would be underpowered. The bias ratio metric ($4.97\times$) was initially computed on a single condition (Mistral-7B FOLIO, $n = 204$); cross-model replication (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}) confirms the pattern but is limited to FOLIO. Four-seed replication shows 2 of 4 seeds yielding null or negative results, and the intermediate-SC region is narrow (SC $\approx$ 50--55\%). The process-level similarity metric uses TF-IDF representations (a lower bound on true process similarity). Pairwise Pearson $r$ between traces (mean $0.625$, Mistral-7B) is monotonically related to Fleiss' $\kappa$ across all four model families, supporting $\kappa$ as a valid consensus proxy despite the simplifying assumptions of Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}.
```

</details>

```json
    "oldString": "NLI is effective only when the task naturally decomposes into premise-conclusion entailment.",
    "newString": "NLI is effective only when the task naturally decomposes into premise-conclusion entailment. Even on compatible tasks, NLI provides no benefit when SC accuracy exceeds verifier standalone accuracy (Qwen3-14B ProofWriter: SC 85\\% vs.\\ DeBERTa 36\\%, $\\Delta \\leq +0.50$\\,pp, n.s.), confirming that cross-architecture escape requires verifier competitiveness, not merely architectural independence.",
    "originalFile": "\\paragraph{Scale and language.}\nAll models range from 7B to 32B parameters; whether the independence bottleneck persists at larger scales (70B+) or with reasoning-specialized models is untested. All datasets are English-language logical reasoning benchmarks with discrete answer spaces. We do not evaluate trained FOL extractors, external solvers with verified grounding, or multilingual settings. Oracle analysis is limited to FOLIO and ProofWriter (gold annotations available); extending to commonsense reasoning requires constructing comparable gold constraint sets. The cross-architecture NLI verifier (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) provides no benefit on LogiQA (combo $\\Delta \\leq +1$\\,pp, all $p > 0.5$), indicating that cross-architecture escape requires task-verifier alignment---NLI is effective only when the task naturally decomposes into premise-conclusion entailment.\n\n\\paragraph{Statistical power.}\nThe per-label complementarity analysis of the cross-architecture NLI result (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) involves small cell sizes (e.g., 42 \\textsc{False} problems, 69 \\textsc{Unknown} problems); per-label McNemar tests would be underpowered. The bias ratio metric ($4.97\\times$) was initially computed on a single condition (Mistral-7B FOLIO, $n = 204$); cross-model replication (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}) confirms the pattern but is limited to FOLIO. Four-seed replication shows 2 of 4 seeds yielding null or negative results, and the intermediate-SC region is narrow (SC $\\approx$ 50--55\\%). The process-level similarity metric uses TF-IDF representations (a lower bound on true process similarity). Pairwise Pearson $r$ between traces (mean $0.625$, Mistral-7B) is monotonically related to Fleiss' $\\kappa$ across all four model families, supporting $\\kappa$ as a valid consensus proxy despite the simplifying assumptions of Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}.\n\n\\paragraph{Computational cost trade-off.}\nSICA requires $4.5\\times$ the wall-clock time of SC (81.4 vs.\\ 17.7\\,min for 204 questions at $K{=}12$), with constraint extraction accounting for 76\\% of overhead (\\S\\ref{sec:exp-setup}). This $3.5\\times$ additional cost yields no significant accuracy gain on any condition, making the method impractical in its current form. Alternative prompting strategies (few-shot, chain-of-thought extraction) might improve extraction quality, though the oracle gap suggests the bottleneck is structural. Additional limitations regarding power analysis and deduplication strategy are discussed in Appendix~\\ref{app:limitations-extended}.\n\n\\paragraph{Reproducibility.} All code, data, and experimental scripts will be released upon publication to facilitate replication of SICA, the oracle controls, and all remediation experiments.\n",
```

[severity: warning] D116 "Aggregation Ceiling" Pivot 审查

## Pivot 评估

从 "SICA fails" 到 "Aggregation Ceiling" 是一个**有力的叙事升级**——将 specific method failure 提升为 general insight about test-time aggregation limits。数据支撑：

| Model | Single-trace | SC@12 | Agg Gain |
|-------|-------------|-------|----------|
| Mistral-7B | 46.4% | 54.4% | +8.0pp |
| Qwen3-NT | 79.4% | 83.8% | +4.5pp |
| Qwen3-T | 85.4% | 86.3% | +0.9pp |

monotonic decrease 清晰。但：

**[warning] 3 个数据点不足以 claim "monotonic" 或 "ceiling"**

3 个模型的 capability 范围 (46-85%) 构成一条趋势线，但：
- 没有 intermediate 点（如 60-70% range 的模型）
- Qwen3-NT 和 Qwen3-T 是同一模型的两个 mode，不是独立模型
- "Monotonic" 需要更多数据点或理论支撑

建议措辞：**"observed trend"** 而非 **"ceiling law"**。3 个点是 suggestive observation，不是 established law。

**[warning] 理论框架再次膨胀：5 Props + 2 Corollaries**

此前协议：Props 2-4 移除，P1 降级为 empirical bound。现在有 5 Propositions + 2 Corollaries = 7 个 formal claims。在 8pp 中放 7 个 Propositions 需要大量空间，且每个都需要足够的证据支撑。

审稿人看到 7 个 propositions 会逐个检验。任何一个不够严谨都会被攻击。建议：最多保留 3 个最强的（BR, Eff-K, Aggregation Ceiling），其余降级为 empirical observations。

**[info] NLI Escape 矩阵的诚实呈现**

NLI scope = "弱模型 + entailment-aligned task only" 的限定很好。这是 honest reporting，审稿人会认可。

## 时间线合理性

全部实验完成 → 论文写作冲刺。5 天内（May 21-25）完成 V9 draft + 审稿迭代 + 定稿。时间上可行，但 "Aggregation Ceiling" 是全新 framing，需要从头写 §6 + 调整 Abstract/Intro/Conclusion/Title——这比 incremental fix 工作量大很多。

建议：如果 Paper Agent 在 May 22 前无法产出 coherent V9 draft，fallback 到 V2 framing（诊断 paper + NLI PoC），不要两头不到岸。

I explicitly rejected V9 Aggregation Ceiling restructuring. Adding a new table and paragraph to §6 is scope creep. I only authorized a single parenthetical reference to appendix for multi-verifier results.


```text
2. **Patch 2 (Abstract)**: Changed NLI numbers from w=3 +3.50pp to w=5 +10.67pp. This is concerning because the paper explicitly says w=3 is the primary finding and w=5 risks overfitting, so switching the headline number to w=5 undermines our own caveat. 3. **Patch 3 (Introduction)**: Same w=5 number swap plus "trained internal reasoning saturates the ceiling" — more V9 framing.

The core problems are the w=3 → w=5 headline swap contradicting our own overfitting warning, the new table in §6 main text when I explicitly said no new sections or tables, and the aggregation ceiling framing that I already rejected. I need to revert the w=5 numbers back to w=3 in the Abstract and Introduction, then decide whether the §6 table should stay or be moved to the appendix.

Looking at the page count, the main text is exactly 8 pages with the new table included, so it technically fits. But the w=5 result is statistically significant at +10.67pp with p<0.001 even after Benjamini-Hochberg correction, whereas w=3 gives +3.50pp with p=0.013 (BH adjusted p=0.038). The original framing was to report w=3 as primary because higher weights risk overfitting, so using w=5 as the headline number creates a real inconsistency that reviewers will catch.

I'm settling on keeping the multi-verifier claim across three NLI architectures since that's supported by the Dir-J3 results, reverting the w=3 to w=5 change in the abstract and intro to stay consistent with the overfitting concern, and dropping the "aggregation ceiling" and "trained reasoning internalization" language that crept in from the V9 version. The new §6 table and capability gap hypothesis paragraph are worth keeping if they fit within the page limit, but I should verify against the actual compiled files first. ```text
```
```text
1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	The analyses in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}--\S\ref{sec:remediation} confirm all three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\S\ref{sec:mirage}): self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's bias and cannot break the same-model ceiling. The oracle gap (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms the aggregation mechanism is sound given uncontaminated constraints; the corrective signal must come from outside the model's distribution---escaping the mirage entirely.
6	
7	\paragraph{Method.}
8	We test this with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M parameters, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}. For each problem, DeBERTa classifies the (premise, conclusion) pair into \textsc{entailment}, \textsc{contradiction}, or \textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via a weight parameter $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.
9	
10	\paragraph{Main result.}
11	DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode at $w{=}1$ (NLI as 13th voter) reaches 57.84\% ($\Delta = +1.96$\,pp, McNemar exact $p = 0.125$, n.s.; $b{=}0$, $c{=}4$); $w{=}3$ yields 58.82\% ($+2.94$\,pp, $p = 0.210$, n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}1$ yields $+0.83$\,pp (n.s.), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\,pp ($p = 0.013$), and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$). Notably, same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter performance ($-2.83$\,pp, $p = 0.014$; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), while the cross-architecture combo yields significant improvement---illustrating the Symbolic Mirage prediction (\S\ref{sec:mirage}) that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution. We report $w{=}3$ on ProofWriter as the primary finding; higher weights risk overfitting. After Benjamini--Hochberg correction for six comparisons ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adjusted $p = 0.038$) and $w{=}5$ (adjusted $p < 0.001$); all four FOLIO comparisons are non-significant. A per-label caveat: DeBERTa over-predicts \textsc{False} on ProofWriter (67.8\% of predictions) and performs poorly on \textsc{Unknown} (3.5\% accuracy), mirroring the FOLIO pattern where NLI's ``neutral'' fails to capture logical unknowability.
12	
13	\paragraph{Why it works.}
14	The 406M encoder-only DeBERTa and 7B decoder-only Mistral share no architecture, training data, or inference mechanism, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). This produces per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \textsc{False} problems (71.43\% vs.\ SC's 38.10\%) where the LLM is weakest, while SC dominates \textsc{Unknown} (68.12\% vs.\ 18.84\%). All three NLI architectures tested---DeBERTa, RoBERTa, and BART (all MNLI-trained)---produce significant improvements on ProofWriter at $w \geq 3$ (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}), confirming the effect is not verifier-specific.
15	
16	\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}
17	Cross-architecture verification succeeds when the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC baseline on the target task. On ProofWriter, DeBERTa standalone (55.00\%) exceeds Mistral-7B SC (39.33\%), enabling the combo to reach $+10.67$\,pp at $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$). On Qwen3-14B FOLIO, where SC (86.27\%) far exceeds DeBERTa standalone (49.51\%), the combo yields $\Delta \leq +0.49$\,pp (all $p = 1.0$)---the verifier signal is overwhelmed. Table~\ref{tab:agg-gain} quantifies the underlying mechanism: aggregation gain itself shrinks monotonically with model capability, from $+8.01$\,pp (Mistral-7B) to $+0.86$\,pp (Qwen3-14B thinking mode; see Appendix~\ref{app:single-trace} for full per-$K$ statistics). This suggests that trained internal reasoning \citep{guo2025deepseekr1, qwen2024qwq} saturates the aggregation ceiling, leaving progressively less room for any test-time method---symbolic or otherwise---to improve.
18	
19	\begin{table}[t]
20	\centering
21	\small
22	\caption{Aggregation gain shrinks with model capability. Single-trace accuracy averaged over $K{=}12$ traces ($\pm$ std). All on FOLIO ($n{=}204$).}
23	\label{tab:agg-gain}
24	\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrr@{}}
25	\toprule
26	Model & Single (\%) & SC@12 (\%) & Gain (pp) \\
27	\midrule
28	Mistral-7B & 46.41\,$\pm$\,2.80 & 54.41 & $+$8.01 \\
29	Qwen3-14B & 79.37\,$\pm$\,1.98 & 83.82 & $+$4.45 \\
30	Qwen3-14B (think) & 85.42\,$\pm$\,0.99 & 86.27 & $+$0.86 \\
31	\bottomrule
32	\end{tabular}
33	\end{table}
34
```

### Tool result

_source line 4462 · 2026-05-20T18:29:45.467Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01F1jAksk2orXTQBqFbzbjPC`</summary>

```text
1	Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.
2	We investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic (FOL) constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.
3	Across the full LLM capability range---from Mistral-7B (54\% SC) to Qwen3-14B thinking mode (86\% SC)---and three reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect; TOST equivalence tests (margin $\pm 2$\,pp) establish equivalence for four conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.
4	We identify \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC.
5	Process-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\kappa$ ($0.41$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.
6	Twenty-plus same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\kappa \geq 0.40$ across three model families).
7	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp and $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of the oracle ceiling.
8	However, a cross-architecture proof-of-concept---DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC---yields $+10.67$\,pp on ProofWriter at $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures, suggesting that the oracle gap is partially recoverable when verification signal comes from outside the reasoning model's distribution. Aggregation gain itself shrinks monotonically from $+8.01$\,pp (Mistral-7B) to $+0.86$\,pp (Qwen3-14B thinking mode), motivating a shift from test-time aggregation to trained reasoning internalization.
9	
```

```text
1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Test-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.
4	The dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.
5	SC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.
6	\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.
7	The gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.
8	
9	Confidence-weighted \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.
10	Recent neuro-symbolic methods \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.
11	A simpler question precedes these: can the \emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?
12	No prior work has systematically evaluated this.
13	
14	We ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \citep{austensmith1996}.
15	When independence fails \citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.
16	Measuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
17	The bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.
18	Chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.41$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
19	When the same model extracts FOL constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
20	Self-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.
21	
22	We study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract FOL constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3} (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
23	We evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances) with four-seed replication (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}).
24	Twenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families---from prompt engineering through method-level redesign---all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck is persistent at the 7B--32B scale.
25	While prior work qualitatively concludes that LLMs cannot self-correct or self-verify without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics (Effective-$K$, bias ratio), show that formal Z3-checked verification also fails, and reveal process-level reasoning homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.
26	
27	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound: same-model gold FOL yields $+29.90$\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+14.22$\,pp (Qwen-14B) on FOLIO ($p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of the same-model ceiling (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
28	The central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15 (tripling Eff-$K$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp; SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG all cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that holds across three model families (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).
29	A cross-architecture proof-of-concept suggests this gap is recoverable: DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC yields $+10.67$\,pp on ProofWriter at $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
30	Aggregation gain itself shrinks from $+8.01$\,pp (Mistral-7B) to $+0.86$\,pp (Qwen3-14B thinking mode), suggesting that trained internal reasoning (o1, DeepSeek-R1, Qwen3 thinking) saturates the ceiling that test-time aggregation exploits.
31	
32	Our contributions are:
33	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
34	\item \textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\kappa \geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-plus remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier ($+10.67$\,pp on ProofWriter, $p < 0.001$) suggests the ceiling is escapable (\S\ref{sec:remediation}, \S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
35	
36	\item \textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
37	
38	\item \textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four model families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$); the gap intensifies with model capability, showing reasoning homogeneity is architecture-independent (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
39	\end{enumerate}
40	
```

```json
    "originalFile": "Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.\nWe investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic (FOL) constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.\nAcross the full LLM capability range---from Mistral-7B (54\\% SC) to Qwen3-14B thinking mode (86\\% SC)---and three reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect;     "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
```
```json
    "oldString": "A cross-architecture proof-of-concept suggests this gap is recoverable: DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC yields $+10.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter at $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\nAggregation gain itself shrinks from $+8.01$\\,pp (Mistral-7B) to $+0.86$\\,pp (Qwen3-14B thinking mode), suggesting that trained internal reasoning (o1, DeepSeek-R1, Qwen3 thinking) saturates the ceiling that test-time aggregation exploits.",
    "newString": "A cross-architecture proof-of-concept suggests this gap is recoverable: DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts FOL constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract FOL constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nWe evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances) with four-seed replication (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nTwenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families---from prompt engineering through method-level redesign---all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck is persistent at the 7B--32B scale.\nWhile prior work qualitatively concludes that LLMs cannot self-correct or self-verify without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics (Effective-$K$, bias ratio), show that formal Z3-checked verification also fails, and reveal process-level reasoning homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.\n\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound: same-model gold FOL yields $+29.90$\\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+14.22$\\,pp (Qwen-14B) on FOLIO ($p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the same-model ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15 (tripling Eff-$K$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp; SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG all cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that holds across three model families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept suggests this gap is recoverable: DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC yields $+10.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter at $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\nAggregation gain itself shrinks from $+8.01$\\,pp (Mistral-7B) to $+0.86$\\,pp (Qwen3-14B thinking mode), suggesting that trained internal reasoning (o1, DeepSeek-R1, Qwen3 thinking) saturates the ceiling that test-time aggregation exploits.\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-plus remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier ($+10.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter, $p < 0.001$) suggests the ceiling is escapable (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four model families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$); the gap intensifies with model capability, showing reasoning homogeneity is architecture-independent (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts FOL constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract FOL constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nWe evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances) with four-seed replication (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nTwenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families---from prompt engineering through method-level redesign---all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck is persistent at the 7B--32B scale.\nWhile prior work qualitatively concludes that LLMs cannot self-correct or self-verify without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics (Effective-$K$, bias ratio), show that formal Z3-checked verification also fails, and reveal process-level reasoning homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.\n\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound: same-model gold FOL yields $+29.90$\\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+14.22$\\,pp (Qwen-14B) on FOLIO ($p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the same-model ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15 (tripling Eff-$K$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp; SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG all cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that holds across three model families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept suggests this gap is recoverable: DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-plus remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier ($+10.67$\\,pp on ProofWriter, $p < 0.001$) suggests the ceiling is escapable (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four model families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$); the gap intensifies with model capability, showing reasoning homogeneity is architecture-independent (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
```

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\nThe analyses in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}--\\S\\ref{sec:remediation} confirm all three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}): self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's bias and cannot break the same-model ceiling. The oracle gap (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms the aggregation mechanism is sound given uncontaminated constraints; the corrective signal must come from outside the model's distribution---escaping the mirage entirely.\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test this with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M parameters, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}. For each problem, DeBERTa classifies the (premise, conclusion) pair into \\textsc{entailment}, \\textsc{contradiction}, or \\textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via a weight parameter $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode at $w{=}1$ (NLI as 13th voter) reaches 57.84\\% ($\\Delta = +1.96$\\,pp, McNemar exact $p = 0.125$, n.s.; $b{=}0$, $c{=}4$); $w{=}3$ yields 58.82\\% ($+2.94$\\,pp, $p = 0.210$, n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}1$ yields $+0.83$\\,pp (n.s.), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\\,pp ($p = 0.013$), and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$). Notably, same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter performance ($-2.83$\\,pp, $p = 0.014$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), while the cross-architecture combo yields significant improvement---illustrating the Symbolic Mirage prediction (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}) that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution. We report $w{=}3$ on ProofWriter as the primary finding; higher weights risk overfitting. After Benjamini--Hochberg correction for six comparisons ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adjusted $p = 0.038$) and $w{=}5$ (adjusted $p < 0.001$); all four FOLIO comparisons are non-significant. A per-label caveat: DeBERTa over-predicts \\textsc{False} on ProofWriter (67.8\\% of predictions) and performs poorly on \\textsc{Unknown} (3.5\\% accuracy), mirroring the FOLIO pattern where NLI's ``neutral'' fails to capture logical unknowability.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nThe 406M encoder-only DeBERTa and 7B decoder-only Mistral share no architecture, training data, or inference mechanism, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). All three NLI architectures tested---DeBERTa, RoBERTa, and BART (all MNLI-trained)---produce significant improvements on ProofWriter at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}), confirming the effect is not verifier-specific.\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\nCross-architecture verification succeeds when the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC baseline on the target task. On ProofWriter, DeBERTa standalone (55.00\\%) exceeds Mistral-7B SC (39.33\\%), enabling the combo to reach $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$). On Qwen3-14B FOLIO, where SC (86.27\\%) far exceeds DeBERTa standalone (49.51\\%), the combo yields $\\Delta \\leq +0.49$\\,pp (all $p = 1.0$)---the verifier signal is overwhelmed. Table~\\ref{tab:agg-gain} quantifies the underlying mechanism: aggregation gain itself shrinks monotonically with model capability, from $+8.01$\\,pp (Mistral-7B) to $+0.86$\\,pp (Qwen3-14B thinking mode; see Appendix~\\ref{app:single-trace} for full per-$K$ statistics). This suggests that trained internal reasoning \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1, qwen2024qwq} saturates the aggregation ceiling, leaving progressively less room for any test-time method---symbolic or otherwise---to improve.",
          "+Cross-architecture verification succeeds when the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC baseline on the target task. On ProofWriter, DeBERTa standalone (55.00\\%) exceeds Mistral-7B SC (39.33\\%), enabling the combo to reach $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$). On Qwen3-14B FOLIO, where SC (86.27\\%) far exceeds DeBERTa standalone (49.51\\%), the combo yields $\\Delta \\leq +0.49$\\,pp (all $p = 1.0$)---the verifier signal is overwhelmed. Table~\\ref{tab:agg-gain} quantifies the underlying mechanism: aggregation gain shrinks with model capability, from $+8.01$\\,pp (Mistral-7B) to $+0.86$\\,pp (Qwen3-14B thinking mode; see Appendix~\\ref{app:single-trace} for full per-$K$ statistics), suggesting that stronger internal reasoning leaves progressively less room for test-time aggregation methods to improve.",
          " ",
          " \\begin{table}[t]",
          " \\centering"
        ]
      }
    ],
```

```text
1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Test-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.
4	The dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.
5	SC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.
6	\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.
7	This complements recent work on internalized reasoning (e.g., reasoning-enhanced models such as Qwen3 thinking-mode), where capability improvements occur within the model rather than at aggregation time.
8	The gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.
9	
10	Confidence-weighted \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.
11	Recent neuro-symbolic methods \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.
12	A simpler question precedes these: can the \emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?
13	No prior work has systematically evaluated this.
14	
15	We ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \citep{austensmith1996}.
16	When independence fails \citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.
17	Measuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
18	The bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.
19	Chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.41$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
20	When the same model extracts FOL constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
21	Self-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.
22	
23	We study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract FOL constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3} (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
24	We evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances) with four-seed replication (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}).
25	Twenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families---from prompt engineering through method-level redesign---all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck is persistent at the 7B--32B scale.
26	While prior work qualitatively concludes that LLMs cannot self-correct or self-verify without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics (Effective-$K$, bias ratio), show that formal Z3-checked verification also fails, and reveal process-level reasoning homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.
27	
28	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound: same-model gold FOL yields $+29.90$\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+14.22$\,pp (Qwen-14B) on FOLIO ($p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of the same-model ceiling (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
29	The central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15 (tripling Eff-$K$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp; SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG all cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that holds across three model families (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).
30	A cross-architecture proof-of-concept suggests this gap is recoverable: DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC yields $+3.50$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
31	
32	Our contributions are:
33	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
34	\item \textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\kappa \geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-plus remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier ($+3.50$\,pp on ProofWriter, $p = 0.013$; confirmed across three NLI architectures) suggests the ceiling is escapable (\S\ref{sec:remediation}, \S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
35	
36	\item \textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
37	
38	\item \textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four model families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$); the gap intensifies with model capability, showing reasoning homogeneity is architecture-independent (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
39	\end{enumerate}
40	
```

### Tool result

_source line 4506 · 2026-05-20T18:35:45.886Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01KHKXSwTpY7qYrDQSUsbzfQ`</summary>

```text
1	Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.
2	We investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic (FOL) constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.
3	Across the full LLM capability range---from Mistral-7B (54\% SC) to Qwen3-14B thinking mode (86\% SC)---and three reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect; TOST equivalence tests (margin $\pm 2$\,pp) establish equivalence for four conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.
4	We identify \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC.
5	Process-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\kappa$ ($0.41$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.
6	Twenty-plus same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\kappa \geq 0.40$ across three model families).
7	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp and $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of the oracle ceiling.
8	However, a cross-architecture proof-of-concept---DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC---yields $+3.50$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures, suggesting that the oracle gap is partially recoverable when verification signal comes from outside the reasoning model's distribution.
9	Aggregation gain depends on the gap between verifier and generator capability, suggesting that improvements in base-model capability narrow the space for test-time aggregation.
10	
```

```json
    "originalFile": "Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.\nWe investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts first-order logic (FOL) constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.\nAcross the full LLM capability range---from Mistral-7B (54\\% SC) to Qwen3-14B thinking mode (86\\% SC)---and three reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect; TOST equivalence tests (margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp) establish equivalence for four conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.\nWe identify \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC.\nProcess-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ($0.41$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.\nTwenty-plus same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across three model families).\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp and $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the oracle ceiling.\nHowever, a cross-architecture proof-of-concept---DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC---yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; {
```
```json
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "f80fcee9-6115-48ce-8e54-92c6e546579e",

    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
    "oldString": "\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThis complements recent work on internalized reasoning (e.g., reasoning-enhanced models such as Qwen3 thinking-mode), where capability improvements occur within the model rather than at aggregation time.\nThe gap between",
    "newString": "\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThis complements recent work on internalized reasoning (e.g., reasoning-enhanced models such as Qwen3 thinking-mode), where capability improvements occur within the model rather than at aggregation time.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts FOL constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract FOL constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nWe evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances) with four-seed replication (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nTwenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families---from prompt engineering through method-level redesign---all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck is persistent at the 7B--32B scale.\nWhile prior work qualitatively concludes that LLMs cannot self-correct or self-verify without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics (Effective-$K$, bias ratio), show that formal Z3-checked verification also fails, and reveal process-level reasoning homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.\n\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound: same-model gold FOL yields $+29.90$\\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+14.22$\\,pp (Qwen-14B) on FOLIO ($p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the same-model ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15 (tripling Eff-$K$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp; SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG all cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that holds across three model families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept suggests this gap is recoverable: DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-plus remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier ($+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter, $p = 0.013$; confirmed across three NLI architectures) suggests the ceiling is escapable (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four model families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$); the gap intensifies with model capability, showing reasoning homogeneity is architecture-independent (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
```
