<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01WuXq41xqhfkJiQoHmFkUzL`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	The analyses in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}--\S\ref{sec:remediation} confirm all three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\S\ref{sec:mirage}): self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's bias. The oracle gap (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms the mechanism is sound given uncontaminated constraints; the corrective signal must come from outside the model's distribution.
6	
7	\paragraph{Method.}
8	We test this with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs into \textsc{entailment}, \textsc{contradiction}, or \textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via weight $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.
9	
10	\paragraph{Main result.}
11	DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+1.96$\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\,pp ($p = 0.013$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, $p = 0.014$; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary; higher weights risk overfitting. After Benjamini--Hochberg correction ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adjusted $p = 0.038$) and $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$); FOLIO comparisons are non-significant. Per-label caveat: DeBERTa over-predicts \textsc{False} on ProofWriter (67.8\%) and performs poorly on \textsc{Unknown} (3.5\% accuracy), as NLI's ``neutral'' fails to capture logical unknowability.
12	
13	\paragraph{Why it works.}
14	The 406M encoder-only DeBERTa and 7B decoder-only Mistral share no architecture, training data, or inference mechanism, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). This produces per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \textsc{False} problems (71.43\% vs.\ SC's 38.10\%) where the LLM is weakest, while SC dominates \textsc{Unknown} (68.12\% vs.\ 18.84\%). All three NLI architectures tested---DeBERTa, RoBERTa, and BART (all MNLI-trained)---produce significant improvements on ProofWriter at $w \geq 3$ (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}), confirming the effect is not verifier-specific.
15	
16	\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}
17	Cross-architecture verification succeeds when the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC baseline. On ProofWriter, DeBERTa standalone (55.00\%) exceeds Mistral-7B SC (39.33\%), enabling $+10.67$\,pp at $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$). On Qwen3-14B FOLIO, where SC (86.27\%) far exceeds DeBERTa (49.51\%), the combo yields $\Delta \leq +0.49$\,pp (all $p = 1.0$). Aggregation gain shrinks with model capability: $+8.01$\,pp (Mistral-7B) to $+0.86$\,pp (Qwen3-14B thinking mode; Table~\ref{tab:single-trace-stats}). Across all conditions at $w{=}3$, combo gain is perfectly rank-correlated with the capability gap (Spearman $\rho = 1.00$; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}), and a sigmoid fit ($R^2 = 0.97$, inflection at gap~$\approx 1$\,pp; linear $R^2 = 0.87$, $p = 0.020$) reveals a phase transition: meaningful gain emerges only when the verifier exceeds the generator's baseline.
18	
19	\begin{figure}[t]
20	\centering
21	\includegraphics[width=0.82\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}
22	\vspace{-3mm}
23	\caption{Combo gain vs.\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$. Sigmoid fit ($R^2 = 0.97$) shows a phase transition near gap~$\approx 1$\,pp.}
24	\label{fig:capability-gap}
25	\vspace{-5mm}
26	\end{figure}
27

The figure shows 6 data points:

3. Qwen3-14B FOLIO (DeBERTa) - gap ~-37, gain ~0.49
4. Qwen3-14Bthink FOLIO (DeBERTa) - gap ~-37, gain ~0.49


5. RoBERTa-MNLI - gap ~-1, gain ~2.0
6. BART-MNLI - gap ~+7, gain ~3.0

I'm checking the composition: four DeBERTa points in blue, one RoBERTa in red, and one BART in green, with a sigmoid fit overlaid. The y-axis appears to show the gap over single-choice at w=3, though the label is partially cut off.

Now I'm verifying the perfect rank correlation claim with the six points visible. Looking at the ordering by gain: Qwen3 variants tie at the bottom around 0.49, followed by Mistral at 2.94, RoBERTa at 2.0, BART at 3.0, and DeBERTa highest at 3.5. But I notice LogiQA is mentioned in the text but missing from the figure itself.

When I check the gap values against the gains, Mistral and RoBERTa are out of order—Mistral has a larger negative gap but higher gain than RoBERTa, which breaks the monotonic relationship. This means the reported ρ=1.00 couldn't have come from these six points; the Agent must have calculated it from a different subset, possibly just the DeBERTa variants or excluding certain models. points). The figure shows 6 points and the ordering is NOT perfect monotonic.

Actually wait, let me look at the figure more carefully. The Mistral-7B FOLIO point (DeBERTa) has gain ~2.94 at gap ~-5. RoBERTa has gain ~2.0 at gap ~-1. If we rank by gap:
Position 1: gap -37 → gain 0.49 ✓
Position 2: gap -37 → gain 0.49 ✓ (tie)
Position 3: gap -5 → gain 2.94
Position 4: gap -1 → gain 2.0 ← NOT MONOTONIC (should be > 2.94)
Position 5: gap +7 → gain 3.0
Position 6: gap +16 → gain 3.5

So the Spearman ρ can't actually be 1.00 with all these points plotted. The Agent must have computed it on a different subset of the data, which means there's a mismatch between what the text claims and what the figure actually shows. I need to either correct the correlation coefficient to match the full dataset or restrict the figure to only the DeBERTa points that would produce that perfect correlation. I'm settling on keeping Figure 3 since it effectively shows the boundary condition and the paper still fits within the page limit. I'll remove the specific statistical values from the text and instead describe the pattern qualitatively, letting the figure do the heavy lifting. Now I need to update the solutions file accordingly.

The oracle gap (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms the mechanism is sound given uncontaminated constraints; the corrective signal must come from outside the model's distribution.\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test this with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs into \\textsc{entailment}, \\textsc{contradiction}, or \\textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via weight $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+1.96$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\\,pp ($p = 0.013$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, $p = 0.014$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary; higher weights risk overfitting. After Benjamini--Hochberg correction ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adjusted $p = 0.038$) and $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$); FOLIO comparisons are non-significant. Per-label caveat: DeBERTa over-predicts \\textsc{False} on ProofWriter (67.8\\%) and performs poorly on \\textsc{Unknown} (3.5\\% accuracy), as NLI's ``neutral'' fails to capture logical unknowability.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nThe 406M encoder-only DeBERTa and 7B decoder-only Mistral share no architecture, training data, or inference mechanism, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). This produces per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} problems (71.43\\% vs.\\ SC's 38.10\\%) where the LLM is weakest, while SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures tested---DeBERTa, RoBERTa, and BART (all MNLI-trained)---produce significant improvements on ProofWriter at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}), confirming the effect is not verifier-specific.\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\nCross-architecture verification succeeds when the verifier's standalone accuracy exceeds the generator's SC baseline. On ProofWriter, DeBERTa standalone (55.00\\%) exceeds Mistral-7B SC (39.33\\%), enabling $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$). On Qwen3-14B FOLIO, where SC (86.27\\%) far exceeds DeBERTa (49.51\\%), the combo yields $\\Delta \\leq +0.49$\\,pp (all $p = 1.0$). Aggregation gain shrinks with model capability: $+8.01$\\,pp (Mistral-7B) to $+0.86$\\,pp (Qwen3-14B thinking mode; Table~\\ref{tab:single-trace-stats}). Across all conditions at $w{=}3$, combo gain is perfectly rank-correlated with the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 1.00$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}), and a sigmoid fit ($R^2 = 0.97$, inflection at gap~$\\approx 1$\\,pp; linear $R^2 = 0.87$, $p = 0.020$) reveals a phase transition: meaningful gain emerges only when the verifier exceeds the generator's baseline.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.82\\columnwidth]{figures/capability_gap_scatter}\n\\vspace{-3mm}\n\\caption{Combo gain vs.\\ capability gap (verifier standalone $-$ generator SC) at $w{=}3$. Sigmoid fit ($R^2 = 0.97$) shows a phase transition near gap~$\\approx 1$\\,pp.}\n\\label{fig:capability-gap}\n\\vspace{-5mm}\n\\end{figure}\n",
